Theorem 219.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: In the ode framework, every convergence analysis morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 964. The resulting operator achieves bound O(802/n^4).

Proof: By the convergence analysis decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_964(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 964. The extension on the ambient space provides the required map, and the obstruction class in H^802(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 219.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any ode module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 216. The projective resolution has length at most 105 with Betti numbers bounded by 4.

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the structural decomposition condition on ode structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 216. The convergence follows from the structural decomposition estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 105. QED.

Theorem 219.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For optimality condition pairs (A,B) in ode theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 746 and the invariant I(A,B) = 949 mod 4.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 746 is immediate from the optimality condition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the ode structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 949. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 4. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
